Face 98 is a **strip-mesh triangle: 15mm along the pipe axis × 0.25mm around the tube** — the same extreme-aspect disease as the rounded-cube fillet, but now it breaks the jet differently: a skinny chord-triangle's *own plane normal is ~4° off the true surface tangent*, so the jet's `z'` measurements are dominated by frame tilt and scatter. The tensor handled strips fine (normals encode curvature direction), but the jet's frame trusts the facet normal.

The fix: **derive the frame from the point cloud itself (PCA), not from the facet normal** — the smallest-variance direction of the neighborhood *is* the local normal, accurate even for skinny strips:

**Tool: edit**

**Input:**
```json
{
  "filePath": "/Users/sethml/src/brepper/src/stage2/edges.rs",
  "newString": "/// Fit the quadratic height field `z = a·u² + b·u·w + c·w² + d·u + e·w + g`\n/// and return the principal curvatures (eigenvalues of the Hessian\n/// [[2a, b],[b, 2c]]).\n///\n/// The tangent frame comes from a **PCA of the point cloud, not the facet\n/// normal**: a long skinny strip triangle's own plane is a chord whose\n/// normal can sit several degrees off the true surface tangent, which\n/// poisons the height measurement; the smallest-variance direction of the\n/// neighborhood points IS the local normal and stays accurate on strips\n/// and slivers (sign aligned with the face normal hint `z_hint`).\n///\n/// Weighted least squares with a tiny Tikhonov ridge (unobserved\n/// directions — e.g. across a one-facet-wide strip — collapse to ~0\n/// curvature instead of producing garbage), followed by JET_IRLS_ROUNDS of\n/// MAD-based outlier rejection (vertices lying across a junction deviate\n/// from the anchored surface by ~the surface mismatch and are dropped).\n/// `eps` is the absolute residual floor (position noise scale).\nfn jet_curvature(\n    origin: &[f64; 3],\n    z_hint: &[f64; 3],\n    _u_hint: &[f64; 3],\n    points: &[JetPoint],\n    eps: f64,\n) -> Option<(f64, f64)> {\n    // PCA frame: weighted covariance of the points around the origin; the\n    // two largest-variance directions span the tangent plane.\n    let mut wsum = 0.0;\n    let mut cov = nalgebra::Matrix3::<f64>::zeros();\n    for p in points {\n        let d = [\n            p.pos[0] - origin[0],\n            p.pos[1] - origin[1],\n            p.pos[2] - origin[2],\n        ];\n        wsum += p.weight;\n        for a in 0..3 {\n            for b in 0..3 {\n                cov[(a, b)] += p.weight * d[a] * d[b];\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    if wsum <= 0.0 {\n        return None;\n    }\n    cov /= wsum;\n    let eigen = nalgebra::SymmetricEigen::new(cov);\n    // Columns of `eigenvectors`, ascending eigenvalues in nalgebra's\n    // SymmetricEigen? nalgebra does not guarantee order — find them.\n    let mut order = [0usize, 1, 2];\n    order.sort_by_key(|&i| {\n        let v = eigen.eigenvalues[i];\n        ordered_float_key(v)\n    });\n    let (i1, i2, i3) = (order[2], order[1], order[0]);\n    let u = nalgebra::Vector3::new(\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(0, i1)],\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(1, i1)],\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(2, i1)],\n    );\n    let w = nalgebra::Vector3::new(\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(0, i2)],\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(1, i2)],\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(2, i2)],\n    );\n    let mut z_axis = nalgebra::Vector3::new(\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(0, i3)],\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(1, i3)],\n        eigen.eigenvectors[(2, i3)],\n    );\n    if z_axis.dot(&nalgebra::Vector3::from_column_slice(z_hint)) < 0.0 {\n        z_axis = -z_axis;\n    }\n    let (u, w, z_axis) = ([u.x, u.y, u.z], [w.x, w.y, w.z], [z_axis.x, z_axis.y, z_axis.z]);",
  "oldString": "/// Fit the quadratic height field `z = a·u² + b·u·w + c·w² + d·u + e·w + g`\n/// over the tangent frame (`origin`, `z_axis`, `u_hint`) and return the\n/// principal curvatures (eigenvalues of the Hessian [[2a, b],[b, 2c]]).\n///\n/// Weighted least squares with a tiny Tikhonov ridge (unobserved\n/// directions — e.g. across a one-facet-wide strip — collapse to ~0\n/// curvature instead of producing garbage), followed by JET_IRLS_ROUNDS of\n/// MAD-based outlier rejection (vertices lying across a junction deviate\n/// from the anchored surface by ~the surface mismatch and are dropped).\n/// `eps` is the absolute residual floor (position noise scale).\nfn jet_curvature(\n    origin: &[f64; 3],\n    z_axis: &[f64; 3],\n    u_hint: &[f64; 3],\n    points: &[JetPoint],\n    eps: f64,\n) -> Option<(f64, f64)> {\n    // Tangent frame: u = u_hint projected ⊥ z_axis; w = z × u.\n    let mut u = [\n        u_hint[0] - z_axis[0] * dot3(u_hint, z_axis),\n        u_hint[1] - z_axis[1] * dot3(u_hint, z_axis),\n        u_hint[2] - z_axis[2] * dot3(u_hint, z_axis),\n    ];\n    let ulen = (u[0] * u[0] + u[1] * u[1] + u[2] * u[2]).sqrt();\n    if ulen <= 1e-12 {\n        return None;\n    }\n    for c in &mut u {\n        *c /= ulen;\n    }\n    let w = [\n        z_axis[1] * u[2] - z_axis[2] * u[1],\n        z_axis[2] * u[0] - z_axis[0] * u[2],\n        z_axis[0] * u[1] - z_axis[1] * u[0],\n    ];"
}

ww = D @ w; zz = D @ z\nh = np.abs(np.concatenate([uu, ww])).max()\nus, ws, zs = uu/h, ww/h, zz/h\n\ndef fit(us, ws, zs, W):\n    A = np.stack([us*us, us*ws, ws*ws, us, ws, np.ones_like(us)], axis=1)\n    Aw = A * W[:, None]\n    ata = Aw.T @ A + 1e-9*np.eye(6)\n    atb = Aw.T @ zs\n    return np.linalg.solve(ata, atb)\n\nfor trial in range(3):\n    p = fit(us, ws, zs, W)\n    resid = np.abs((np.stack([us*us, us*ws, ws*ws, us, ws, np.ones_like(us)], axis=1) @ p) - zs)\n    mad = 1.4826*np.median(resid)\n    thr = max(3*mad, 1e-7)\n    print(f\"round {trial}: params={np.round(p,4)}\")\n    print(\"  resid:\", np.round(resid,4), \"thr:\", round(thr,4))\n    a,b,c = -2*p[0], -p[1], -2*p[2]\n    mid = (a+c)/2; diff = np.sqrt(((a-c)/2)**2 + b*b)\n    print(\"  k:\", np.round((mid+diff)/h, 4), np.round((mid-diff)/h, 4))\n    idx = np.argsort(resid)\n    keep = np.array([resid[i] <= thr or r < 8 for r, i in enumerate(idx)])\n    if keep.all(): break\n    us, ws, zs, W = us[idx[keep]], ws[idx[keep]], zs[idx[keep]], W[idx[keep]]\nEOF"

  "newString": "    // PCA frame: weighted covariance of the frame-eligible points around\n    // the origin; the two largest-variance directions span the tangent\n    // plane. Own vertices dominate (weight OWN_WEIGHT), so the frame\n    // tracks the face's own surface even when some ring points are wild.\n    let mut wsum = 0.0;\n    let mut cov = nalgebra::Matrix3::<f64>::zeros();\n    for p in points {\n        if !p.in_frame {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let d = [\n            p.pos[0] - origin[0],\n            p.pos[1] - origin[1],\n            p.pos[2] - origin[2],\n        ];\n        wsum += p.weight;\n        for a in 0..3 {\n            for b in 0..3 {\n                cov[(a, b)] += p.weight * d[a] * d[b];\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    if wsum <= 0.0 {\n        return None;\n    }\n    cov /= wsum;",
  "oldString": "    // PCA frame: weighted covariance of the points around the origin; the\n    // two largest-variance directions span the tangent plane.\n    let mut wsum = 0.0;\n    let mut cov = nalgebra::Matrix3::<f64>::zeros();\n    for p in points {\n        let d = [\n            p.pos[0] - origin[0],\n            p.pos[1] - origin[1],\n            p.pos[2] - origin[2],\n        ];\n        wsum += p.weight;\n        for a in 0..3 {\n            for b in 0..3 {\n                cov[(a, b)] += p.weight * d[a] * d[b];\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    if wsum <= 0.0 {\n        return None;\n    }\n    cov /= wsum;"
}

427:             }
428:         }
429:     }
430:     points
431: }

204: ### Stage 2: Segmentation & Classification
205: 
206: **Status: this stage is a from-scratch redesign (2026-07-17). Stages 2.1
207: (edge classification) and 2.2 (region extraction) are implemented
208: (`src/stage2/edges.rs`, `src/stage2/regions.rs`), with `--viz-stages=2.1,2.2`
209: overlays; the old BFS pipeline is nerfed in place (`stage2_bfs_pipeline`,
210: uncalled) and 2.3–2.5 are not yet implemented, so `stage2()` stops at the
211: 2.2 frontier with no selected surfaces.** There is no migration flag: the
212: new structure replaces the old outright, and the test corpus is re-enabled
213: model by model as classification matures. Calibration read (2026-07-18,
214: after the curvature-estimator rewrite): all sharp-edged analytic models,
215: the capsule (fine AND coarse), the pipe-elbow torus bend (one region — the
216: strip problem is structurally gone), and rounded_cube_10_r2_fine (26 true
217: regions: 8 spheres + 12 fillets + 6 planes) partition correctly. Remaining
218: known gaps, all in the repair loop's designed territory: junction-zone
219: confetti at tangent junctions and triple points (filleted_* models,
220: nosecone, cone_tangent_planes — small regions whose rings conflict), and
221: coarse tessellations (rounded_cube_coarse/medium) where descriptor noise
222: approaches the fire threshold; cone-row σ_max gradients near apexes are
223: the expected over-fire case for merge repair.
224: 
225: **Pipeline:** classify mesh edges (2.1) → extract connected regions (2.2) →
226: classify each region against a ladder of surface types (2.3) → repair by
227: split/merge/relabel (2.4) → emit selected surfaces (2.5).
228: 
229: **Precision note**: Mesh vertex positions, normals, and all fitted-surface
230: parameters use double-precision (`f64`) throughout.
231: 
232: #### 2.1 Edge classification
233: 
234: Label every manifold mesh edge INTERIOR or BOUNDARY using fit-free local
235: signals. Region boundaries are a subset of mesh edges (Design Principles #2),
236: so this labeling *is* the segmentation; everything downstream refines it.
237: 
238: **Curvature estimator (implemented 2026-07-18, replacing the original
239: per-edge κ = θ/d descriptor):** each face's descriptor is the pair of
240: principal curvatures `(σ_max, σ_min)` of a symmetric 2×2 tensor S (the
241: shape operator in the face's tangent frame), fitted by least squares from
242: the face's **neighbor normals**: for each edge shared with a neighbor, the
243: first-order identity `S · (c_n − c_f) ≈ n_n − n_f` gives two scalar
244: equations in `(m11, m12, m22)` (a triangle/quad supplies 6–8), using the
245: centroid displacement — exact mesh data — as the direction along which the
246: normal change is measured. The fit uses a **2-ring** of neighbors (through
247: currently-INTERIOR edges only) so that tiny/sliver faces at junction zones
248: are dominated by clean interior evidence of their host surface.
249: 
250: *Why not per-edge κ = θ/d:* on irregular tessellations the centroid distance
251: `d` does not measure arc length in the direction of the normal rotation —
252: e.g. a straight 6 mm fillet section meshed as ONE segment of ~65:1
253: aspect-ratio slivers (rounded_cube_10_r2_fine), where κ came out ~100× too
254: small and randomly varying, fragmenting one surface into ~50 regions. The
255: displacement-based identity attributes curvature to the correct direction
256: regardless of staggering and recovers (1/r, 0) exactly there.
257: 
258: **Classification rules (per edge):**
259: 1. **Sharp**: `|θ_e| > --angular-tolerance` → BOUNDARY (θ_e = dihedral).
260:    Catches every non-tangent junction. The default 25° stays above the
261:    tessellator's maximum dihedral step on smooth surfaces (coarse fillets
262:    step ~24°/row).
263: 2. **Curvature jump** (tangent junctions, G1-smooth but G2-discontinuous):
264:    with `jump(e) = max(|σ_max(f1) − σ_max(f2)|, |σ_min(f1) − σ_min(f2)|)`
265:    and `scale(e) = max(|σ_max|, |σ_min| over both faces)` — the
266:    local-relative criterion, since mesh-global thresholds are dominated by
267:    sharp creases. The rule iterates to a fixpoint with **hysteresis**:
268:    FIRE when `jump > 0.35 · scale` and `jump > κ_floor`; UN-FIRE when
269:    `jump < 0.15 · scale` or `jump < κ_floor`; otherwise keep the previous
270:    state (cap 10 rounds; convergence typically 2–4). Round 1 includes all
271:    non-sharp edges in the neighbor rings, so junction rows are mildly
272:    contaminated (≈1 of 6–8 equations) — enough for the junction to fire
273:    (ratio ≈ 0.6), whereupon round 2+ recomputes cleanly.
274: 3. Everything else → INTERIOR.
275: 
276: `κ_floor = C · ε / d_e²` with ε = f32 position quantization (bbox diagonal ×
277: 1e-7 — STL vertices are f32-exact; NOT `--vertex-tolerance`, whose 5e-5
278: over-floors sliver-dense areas and vetoed real junctions at triple points),
279: `C` calibrated on the corpus.
280: 
281: **Why the two-sided descriptor matters:** `σ_max` alone misses tangent
282: junctions between surfaces sharing their largest curvature — the capsule
283: (pill) model's hemisphere↔cylinder junction has `σ_max = 1/r` on both sides,
284: but `σ_min` jumps from `1/r` (sphere) to `0` (cylinder), which rule 2
285: catches at full strength (verified: both pill_fine and pill_coarse produce
286: exactly 3 regions). Fillet↔plane (`σ_max`: `1/r` vs 0) and
287: fillet↔cylinder with sufficiently different radii fire on `σ_max`. A
288: fillet↔cylinder pair with similar radii (e.g. `1 − r/R < J`) can slip

1: //! Stage 2.1: fit-free edge classification.
2: //!
3: //! Labels every manifold mesh edge INTERIOR or BOUNDARY using two fit-free
4: //! signals (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.1): a sharp dihedral angle, or a jump in the
5: //! per-face two-sided curvature descriptor (k_max, k_min) across the edge.
6: //! The true segmentation's boundaries are a subset of mesh edges
7: //! (tessellators emit seam edges along B-rep face boundaries, even at
8: //! tangencies), so this classification IS the segmentation; stage 2.2
9: //! extracts its connected regions and stage 2.4 (future) repairs mistakes.
10: //!
11: //! **Curvature estimator: per-face anchored osculating-jet fit.** Each
12: //! face's descriptor is the pair of principal curvatures of a quadratic
13: //! height field `z = Q(u,w)` fitted over the face's own tangent plane to
14: //! the vertex *positions* of the face's neighborhood (Cazals & Pouget,
15: //! "Estimating differential quantities using polynomial fitting of
16: //! osculating jets", SGP 2005 — the standard robust curvature estimator
17: //! for irregular meshes). Two properties make it robust to how CAD models
18: //! are actually triangulated:
19: //!
20: //! - **It uses vertex positions** — exact f32 mesh data, one step closer
21: //!   to the true surface than normals — and is agnostic to tessellation
22: //!   layout: aspect-ratio-sliver grids (rounded_cube_10_r2_fine's 65:1
23: //!   fillet triangles), staggered rows, and pole fans all fit the same.
24: //!
25: //! - **The face's own vertices anchor the fit** (weighted OWN_WEIGHT).
26: //!   Tessellators never straddle a B-rep boundary with a triangle, so a
27: //!   face's own corners always lie on its own surface — ground truth for
28: //!   which surface the fit must follow. Neighborhood vertices lying
29: //!   across a junction deviate from the anchored surface by roughly the
30: //!   surface mismatch and are rejected as outliers (MAD-based IRLS);
31: //!   same-surface vertices fit tightly. This is what breaks the
32: //!   deadlock class of the previous neighbor-normal estimators: a
33: //!   junction-adjacent face follows ITS OWN surface instead of averaging
34: //!   two, so the descriptor is boundary-independent and classification
35: //!   runs ONCE — no fixpoint iteration, no starvation, no hysteresis.
36:

(History: per-edge κ = θ/d failed on irregular tessellation; the\n//! neighbor-normal tensor deadlocked on balanced-mix junction rings. The\n//! jet subsumes both.)",
  "oldString": "//! **Curvature estimator: per-face anchored osculating-jet fit.** Each\n//! face's descriptor is the pair of principal curvatures of a quadratic\n//! height field `z = Q(u,w)` fitted over the face's own tangent plane to\n//! the vertex *positions* of the face's neighborhood (Cazals & Pouget,\n//! \"Estimating differential quantities using polynomial fitting of\n//! osculating jets\", SGP 2005 — the standard robust curvature estimator\n//! for irregular meshes). Two properties make it robust to how CAD models\n//! are actually triangulated:\n//!\n//! - **It uses vertex positions** — exact f32 mesh data, one step closer\n//!   to the true surface than normals — and is agnostic to tessellation\n//!   layout: aspect-ratio-sliver grids (rounded_cube_10_r2_fine's 65:1\n//!   fillet triangles), staggered rows, and pole fans all fit the same.\n//!\n//! - **The face's own vertices anchor the fit** (weighted OWN_WEIGHT).\n//!   Tessellators never straddle a B-rep boundary with a triangle, so a\n//!   face's own corners always lie on its own surface — ground truth for\n//!   which surface the fit must follow. Neighborhood vertices lying\n//!   across a junction deviate from the anchored surface by roughly the\n//!   surface mismatch and are rejected as outliers (MAD-based IRLS);\n//!   same-surface vertices fit tightly. This is what breaks the\n//!   deadlock class of the previous neighbor-normal estimators: a\n//!   junction-adjacent face follows ITS OWN surface instead of averaging\n//!   two, so the descriptor is boundary-independent and classification\n//!   runs ONCE — no fixpoint iteration, no starvation, no hysteresis."
}

Done — clippy clean, all 87 lib + 14 segmentation tests pass, working tree contains only the intended changes. Here's the full story.

## Answer to your question: partially expected, but the caps weren't the real problem

The "planar end-cap fragmentation" on stepped_block turned out to be a misattribution — geometry dumps showed the fragmenting faces are **junction-zone faces** (tiny edge fillets at κ≈3 and blend areas between fillets), not the caps themselves. The caps fragment *around* them. But you're right that something deeper was wrong: the fragmentation traced to a curvature estimator that couldn't handle how the mesh was actually triangulated.

## The root cause and the new estimator

Per your push to step back, I replaced the estimator twice before landing on the right one:

1. **Per-edge κ = θ/d** (original): the centroid distance `d` doesn't measure arc length in the direction of the normal rotation on irregular tessellation — 65:1 slivers read ~100× too small, randomly varying.
2. **Neighbor-normal tensor**: handled slivers/strips, but **deadlocked on balanced-mix junction rings** — two compromise-polluted neighbors agree with each other, so no boundary forms, so the contamination never leaves the ring (your stepped_block case).
3. **Anchored osculating jet** (Cazals & Pouget, SGP 2005 — the standard robust answer): per-face quadratic height-field `z = Q(u,w)` fitted to **vertex positions** (exact data) over a **PCA frame** (own+1-ring point cloud, not the facet normal — skinny strip triangles have chord-planes sitting degrees off the true tangent), **anchored on the face's own vertices** (weight 50 — tessellators never straddle seams, so own corners always lie on its own surface). No outlier rejection — I found it forces arbitrary one-side reads that fragment tangent junctions; the anchor alone breaks the symmetry. The jet runs inside a fixpoint iteration with hysteresis so junction rows purify once boundaries fire.

## Results

| Model | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| stepped_block_toroidal_medium | 395 | **260** |
| pipe_elbow | 432 | **35** (bend one region; walls merged at similar-radii outer junction — split-repair case) |
| pill_fine / pill_coarse | 3 / 116 | **3 / 3 true + junction singletons** |
| cone_tangent_planes | ~163 | **11** |
| filleted_cylinder | 78 | **21** |
| rounded_cube_10_r2_fine | 111 | **49 (26 true + small confetti)** |
| all sharp-edged analytic models | ✓ | ✓ unchanged (cube 6, chamfered 26, two_holes 8, …) |

**Remaining confetti is junction-circle/junction-zone faces** — faces on or next to a tangent seam whose neighborhood genuinely spans two surfaces (their descriptor is a real ambiguity, and junction-circle neighborhoods on pill are even planar by construction). That's bounded, small, and is precisely the 2.4 repair loop's designed input (merge by fit-validation).

DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.1 now documents the jet estimator and the calibration read; `tests/stage2_segmentation.rs` (14 tests) pins the structure. Nothing committed. `tmp/dump_regions.rs` (the probe tool) is still on disk — re-add `[[bin]] dump_regions` if you want to use it, e.g. `./target/debug/dump_regions tests/onshape/stepped_block_toroidal_fillets_medium.stl summary`.

Let me think about the assessment content honestly:

**What happened (chronology):**
1. Started from the paused session: partition-then-classify rewrite, stages 2.1 (edges) + 2.2 (regions) landed with per-edge κ = θ/d descriptor.
2. rounded_cube_10_r2_fine over-segmented: one fillet with 65:1 aspect-ratio staggered slivers; κ = θ/d read garbage → fragmentation; giant region merged elsewhere via descriptor noise at triple points.
3. Tried neighbor-normal tensor (S·d = Δn): fixed slivers/strips, got 26 true regions on rounded_cube_fine, pipe_elbow=3, pill=3/3. But deadlocked on stepped_block blend zones (balanced-mix compromise).
4. Replaced with anchored osculating jet (quadratic height-field over PCA frame, own-vertex anchor weight 50). Iterations: outlier rejection (backfired on tangent junction circles), own-scaled rejection (worse), unexplained-dihedral rule (worse), hysteresis fixpoint (good), no rejection (key move — anchor alone breaks symmetry).
5. Final: jet + anchor + PCA frame + no rejection + fixpoint with hysteresis.

**Results:** analytic models exact; pill_fine 3; pill_coarse 3 true + junction singletons; pipe_elbow torus one region + merged walls; cone_tangent 11; filleted_* 21-95; rounded_cube 26 true + confetti; nosecone 130; stepped_block 260.

**Honest evaluation — is segmentation-first working?**

Evidence FOR:
- The core premise holds: on clean regions, curvature descriptors are essentially exact (plane (0,0), cylinder, sphere, torus with correct k1/k2). The pipeline of edge-classify → regions → (future) classify-ladder is sound.
- The pipe-elbow torus arrives as ONE region with no strip machinery — the structural win the redesign promised.
- Junction fragmentation is now bounded to junction-circle/zone confetti (small faces), which is exactly what the repair loop (2.4) is designed to absorb.
- Estimator is now principled (positions, anchored) rather than heuristic.

Evidence AGAINST / open concerns:
- The segmentation is NOT clean before repair: junction circles produce confetti on every tangent junction; coarse meshes are noisy; the similar-radii outer junction (pipe_elbow) misses entirely (torus+cylinders merged).
- A large fraction of the remaining problem (junction-circle faces) is *inherently ambiguous locally* — those faces genuinely belong to the boundary, not to either side. No estimator fixes that; only fit-validation (2.4) can.
- I iterated a LOT on the estimator (5+ variants). Each fixed one failure mode and the corpus guided each fix — mildly heuristic-treadmill-ish, though the final design is principled.

**Is segmentation-first the right architecture, or would the Contingency (seed-first multi-type competition) have been better in hindsight?**

Honest take:
- The partition approach's strength: it gives the classification ladder maximal regions (good fit conditioning — the pipe-elbow torus fit will see the full bend). This is the deep structural advantage and it delivered (torus = 1 region).
- Its weakness: boundary placement at tangent junctions depends on curvature estimation, which is fundamentally noisy at junction rows, and junction-circle faces are locally ambiguous. Boundaries land ±1-2 rows off and confetti results. The repair loop must clean this — 2.4 doesn't exist yet, so this is unproven.
- The contingency (seed-first) places boundaries by fit validation (vertex tolerance), which is exact — no curvature estimation needed for boundaries at all. The same junction would be found exactly. BUT it doesn't solve the strip problem without the degenerate-limit growth model (user's k=1/R idea, unimplemented), and freeform remains second-class, and it keeps more of the old heuristic surface (seed ordering, overlap resolution, acceptance tests).
- In hindsight: the segmentation is NOT obviously better than the contingency for boundary accuracy; its real value is maximal regions for fit conditioning. The contingency is NOT obviously better either: it inherits the conditioning problem (pipe-elbow strips) and doesn't handle freeform.
- The honest answer: they converge. The segmentation's real deliverable is (a) clean region interiors (done — descriptors are exact there) and (b) a partition good enough that repair can finish it (unproven until 2.4 exists). The contingency's deliverable would be exact boundaries at the cost of conditioning machinery. Given the torus result (1 region), segmentation-first looks vindicated for fit conditioning; whether it succeeds depends entirely on whether 2.4 repair can absorb junction confetti — that should be the next thing built and it will be the true test.
- If 2.4 repair proves hard/fragile, the contingency + degenerate-limit growth model is the fallback (and its per-seed fits would benefit from the same maximal-region... no, from local seeds).

Also honest meta-observation: a lot of effort went into edge classification heuristics (fire ratios, hysteresis, floors) — similar in kind to the BFS heuristics the rewrite was meant to escape, though fewer and more principled. The danger sign to watch: if 2.4 needs many knobs, the architecture is re-inventing the treadmill.

**Where the work should go next (for the future LLM):**
1. Stage 2.3 classification ladder (plane rung first — enables coplanar merge), then 2.4 repair (merge: absorb confetti by fit validation; split: re-classify failing regions at lowered J; relabel: ±1 row boundary moves). This is the make-or-break test of the architecture.
2. Planar-cap fragmentation specifically resolves trivially via plane-fit + coplanar merge — good first validation of the repair loop.
3. Junction-circle singletons: absorb by merge (their vertices validate against the neighbor's surface).
4. pipe_elbow similar-radii outer junction: split repair at lowered thresholds inside the merged torus+cylinder region (its classification will fail the ladder → split re-runs 2.1 at J/2 → the outer junction fires there).
5. Cone-row σ_max gradient near apexes (nosecone) → merge repair rejoins same-cone bands.

724: - Compute and report volume using `BRepGProp::VolumeProperties`.
725: - **Multi-shell solids (internal voids)**: after per-shell solids are built, `merge_void_solids` finds solids whose bounding box is contained in a strictly larger solid's box; each such void's shells are added to the container solid (`BRep_Builder::Add`), trying both shell orientations and keeping the one whose resulting volume equals container − Σ voids.
726: 
727: - With the `--compare` flag:
728:     - Count solids in the reference STEP file.
729:     - Compute volumes of both constructed and reference solids. Match by sorted volume. Report a hard **error** if relative volume difference exceeds 1% (a warning until 2026-07-08; a warning-only check let a 46% volume regression slip past a green test suite). Most models achieve volume agreement within ~1e-7 relative difference.
730:     - Compute `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` between each constructed solid and the reference STEP shape. Report error if distance exceeds `--surface-tolerance`.
731: 
732: #### 3.x Design note: shared-edge topological assembly instead of geometric sewing
733: 
734: **Status: IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-08).** Stages 3.4/3.5 above describe the result; this note records the motivation, since it reshaped stage 3's architecture. Stage 3.4 used to build each face independently (`MakeWire`/`MakeFace`/UV-bounds/`ShapeFix` paths) and stage 3.5 reconciled them with `BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing`, a *geometric* stitch that merges edges by proximity within a tolerance. `MakeFace`/`MakeWire`/`ShapeFix` copy edges, so edge sharing was defeated by assembly time and sewing was load-bearing. Every hard case — sphere pole caps, torus seams, cone apex caps, tangent-edge tolerance relaxations — was a symptom of trimming faces independently and matching them by geometric tolerance. The rework made each edge one shared object with per-face pcurves, made seams/degenerate edges first-class topology, and assembled everything with `BRep_Builder`; shells became closed by construction and `cone_tangent_planes` went from 60% volume error to ~1e-7 relative.
735: 
736: **Hard OCCT constraints (established empirically — do not relearn by crashing).**
737: - `ShapeFix_*` can **segfault on cone faces** (pcurve/edge-curve interaction). The assembler attaches pcurves directly with `BRep_Builder` and never routes faces through ShapeFix. (Stage 3.6's `ShapeFix_Solid`/`ShapeFix_Shape` on assembled solids has been safe.)

Honest account of what the\npartition-then-classify rewrite has achieved so far, what it cost, and\nwhether it looks like the right bet — including vs. the\n[Contingency](#contingency-seed-first-multi-type-competition).\n\n### What exists and works\n\n- **Stages 2.1 + 2.2 are implemented and tested** (`src/stage2/edges.rs`,\n  `regions.rs`, `tests/stage2_segmentation.rs` — 14 tests). 2.3–2.5 are\n  NOT implemented; `stage2()` stops at the 2.2 frontier and the old BFS\n  pipeline sits nerfed in place (`stage2_bfs_pipeline`, uncalled) until\n  the deletion pass. **The old stage-2/3/4 integration tests are red by\n  design** — do not \"fix\" them by reviving the old pipeline.\n- **The curvature estimator is now principled, and region interiors are\n  essentially exact.** Per-face anchored osculating-jet: quadratic\n  height-field fitted to vertex *positions* over a PCA frame of the\n  own+1-ring point cloud, anchored on the face's own vertices (weight\n  50), no outlier rejection, inside a hysteresis fixpoint. On clean\n  regions the descriptors are right to a few %: plane (0,0), cylinder\n  (1/r, 0), sphere (1/r, 1/r), torus (1/r_tube, k2(ψ) with the correct\n  sign change at the inner equator), cone (1/ρ(h), 0). Verified directly\n  with `dump_regions` probes on the corpus.\n- **The redesign's headline structural win is real.** The pipe-elbow\n  torus bend arrives as ONE region with zero strip machinery — the\n  strip problem (local seeds can't condition high-DOF fits) is gone,\n  because the partition, not the seed, determines fit support.\n- Measured state of the corpus: all sharp-edged analytic models exact\n  (cube 6, block_with_hole 7, pipe 4, two_holes 8, spherical_pocket 7,\n  chamfered_cube 26); pill_fine 3; pill_coarse 3 true + junction\n  singletons; pipe_elbow bend one region (+ merged walls); cone_tangent\n  11 (was ~160 with earlier estimators); filleted_cylinder 21;\n  filleted_pipe/hole_block ~95; nosecone ~130; stepped_block ~260;\n  rounded_cube_fine 26 true regions + small confetti; rounded_cube\n  medium/coarse ~50-70.\n\n### What was hard, and what it means\n\n- **The estimator was the whole battle** — five variants were tried\n  (per-edge κ=θ/d → neighbor-normal tensor → jet+outlier-rejection →\n  +weights/thresholds → final jet, no rejection). Two durable lessons:\n  (1) use vertex *positions*, not normals — a skinny strip/sliver\n  triangle's plane is a chord whose normal is degrees off the true\n  tangent, poisoning any normal-based estimate; (2) outlier rejection\n  *backfires on tangent junctions* — it forces arbitrary one-side reads\n  that vary face to face and fragment the junction. The own-vertex\n  anchor alone breaks the junction symmetry. Do not re-add rejection.\n- **Junction-circle faces are inherently ambiguous, and no local\n  estimator fixes that.** A face whose vertices sit on a tangent seam\n  genuinely belongs to the boundary, not to either side; on pill the\n  seam-circle neighborhood is even planar by construction, so any\n  position fit reads (0,0). These become singleton regions. This is\n  EXPECTED and is the 2.4 merge repair's designed input — it is not a\n  classifier bug to keep polishing.\n- **Similar-radii tangent junctions still miss.** pipe_elbow's outer\n  torus↔cylinder junction (σ_min jump 0.067/0.2 = 0.33 < FIRE 0.35)\n  doesn't fire, so the cylinder walls merge with the bend. This is the\n  documented \"similar radii\" case for 2.4 split repair, not an oversight.\n- **Knob count is a real risk.** The classifier still has FIRE_RATIO,\n  UNFIRE_RATIO, FLOOR_COEFF, OWN_WEIGHT, MAX_ROUNDS — fewer and more\n  principled than the BFS pipeline's knobs, but the same *kind* of\n  machinery. If stage 2.4 accumulates more, this architecture is\n  re-inventing the heuristic treadmill it was created to escape.\n- The estimator is O(N·ring) per round × ~3 rounds and comfortably fast\n  (rounded_cube_fine 10.8k faces: ~0.2 s; pipe_elbow: ~0.02 s).\n\n### Segmentation-first vs. the Contingency — an honest verdict\n\nThe Contingency (seed-first multi-type competition) places boundaries by\n**fit validation at vertex tolerance** — exact, no curvature estimation\nneeded, so tangent junctions and junction circles come out clean by\nconstruction. Segmentation-first places boundaries by **curvature\nestimation**, which is fundamentally noisy at junction rows and undefined\non junction circles — producing ±1-row boundary error and seam confetti\nthat the (still unwritten) 2.4 repair loop must absorb.\n\nOn the other hand, segmentation-first's **fit support is maximal**:\nclassification fits see whole regions, which is what dissolves the\nstrip problem (the Contingency still has it — from any elbow seed the\ntorus rung is fed a 1-ring and loses to a cylinder strip — unless the\ndegenerate-limit growth model is built too). And segmentation-first\nkeeps freeform first-class (a freeform region is a region; the\nContingency leaves freeform as leftover-gathering).\n\n**Verdict: they converge, and the deciding evidence doesn't exist yet.**\nThe Contingency wins on boundary exactness; segmentation-first wins on\nfit conditioning and freeform. What segmentation-first has already\nproven: region interiors are exact and the strip problem is gone. What\nit has NOT yet proven: that the 2.4 repair loop can absorb the junction\nfringe cheaply and without a new pile of knobs. **Build 2.3/2.4 next —\nthat is the make-or-break test of this architecture.** If repair turns\nout fragile or knob-heavy, retreat to the Contingency (its 2.4 repair\nloop, invariants, and 2.5 output contract carry over unchanged, and the\ndegenerate-limit growth model fixes its strip problem); do NOT spend\nmore effort tuning the edge classifier first.\n\n### What to build next (in order)\n\n1. **Stage 2.3 classification ladder** (`src/stage2/classify.rs`), plane\n   rung first — it enables the coplanar merge and immediately validates\n   the repair loop on planar fragmentation (planar caps fragmented at\n   junction zones merge trivially). Reuse the existing fitters in\n   `planar.rs`/`cylindrical.rs`/`conical.rs`/`spherical.rs`/`toroidal.rs`\n   (their fitting/validation functions; their seeding/BFS entry points\n   go). Terminal per-facet-plane fallback from day one.\n2. **Stage 2.4 repair loop** (`src/stage2/repair.rs`): merge (absorb\n   regions whose vertices validate within `--vertex-tolerance` against an\n   adjacent larger region's surface — this eats the junction-circle\n   singletons and junction-zone confetti), split (re-run 2.1 at lowered\n   thresholds inside regions that fail the whole ladder — this is what\n   recovers pipe_elbow's outer junction and any residual over-merge),\n   boundary relabel (±1 row, the old 2.9 port). Enforce the three\n   invariants (total coverage, connectivity, per-region validity).\n3. Then wire the freeform rung (bspline.rs, gathering deleted), the\n   deletion pass (`bfs.rs`, `stage2_bfs_pipeline`, `--skip-stage`,\n   greedy selection, absorb, MeshFace hypothesis fields), and re-enable\n   the corpus per Rewrite Roadmap step 6.\n4. Useful diagnostics while working: `cargo build --bin dump_regions`\n   (modes: summary, singletons, adj, probe, geom, leaks, rdesc, jet);\n   `--viz-stages=2.1,2.2` shows sharp (red) / jump (orange) edges and\n   region colors.\n\n---\n\n## Contingency: Seed-First Multi-Type Competition",

Committed as `7d66059` on `experiment-fable`. Summary of what's in it and where things stand:

**The commit** (8 files, +1282/−118):
- `src/bin/dump_regions.rs` — now a proper `cargo build` target (moved out of tmp/; modes: summary, singletons, adj, probe, geom, leaks, rdesc, jet)
- `src/stage2/edges.rs` — the anchored osculating-jet estimator (PCA frame, own-vertex anchor weight 50, no rejection, hysteresis fixpoint)
- `src/stage2/{mod,regions}.rs`, `src/bin/brepper.rs` — 2.1/2.2 wired, old pipeline nerfed, stage-3 guard
- `tests/stage2_segmentation.rs` — 14 tests
- `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md` — §2.1 documents the jet; new **Stage-2 Retrospective** section; `TODO.md` points at it

**The retrospective's honest verdict** (in the repo at HEAD for the next session):

- **What's proven**: region interiors are essentially exact (plane, cylinder, sphere, torus — verified directly); the strip problem is structurally gone (pipe-elbow bend = one region, no seed machinery); cone_tangent went ~160 → 11 regions.
- **What's not proven**: that the 2.4 repair loop can absorb the junction fringe (junction-circle faces are inherently ambiguous — no local estimator fixes that; similar-radii outer junctions still miss). **Building 2.3/2.4 next is the make-or-break test of the architecture.**
- **vs. the Contingency**: it converges, and the deciding evidence doesn't exist yet. The Contingency wins on boundary exactness (fit-validation needs no curvature estimation); segmentation-first wins on fit conditioning (maximal regions) and freeform. If repair turns out fragile or knob-heavy, retreat to the Contingency — its machinery carries over — rather than tuning the classifier further. Also flagged: the classifier already has 5 knobs (fire/unfire/floor/own-weight/rounds) — the treadmill risk is real and should be watched.

87 lib + 14 segmentation tests green, clippy clean. The untracked test STLs in `tests/downloaded/` were left alone per repo convention.
